. The expected change in the frequency of
il
-edges is calculated as:

\displaystyle\sum_{\sum_{i^{\prime}=1}^{n}k_{i^{\prime}}=k}\frac{2k_{l}}{kN}\mathcal{P}\left(\Delta p_{il}=\frac{2k_{l}}{kN}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\sum_{i^{\prime}=1}^{n}k_{i^{\prime}}=k}\frac{2k_{l}}{kN}\sum_{j=1,j\neq i}^{n}x_{j}\frac{k!}{\prod_{i^{\prime}=1}^{n}k_{i^{\prime}}!}\left(\prod_{i^{\prime}=1}^{n}{q_{i^{\prime}|j}}^{k_{i^{\prime}}}\right)\mathcal{P}(j\leftarrow i)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\sum_{i^{\prime}=1}^{n}k_{i^{\prime}}=k}\frac{2k_{l}}{kN}\sum_{j=1,j\neq i}^{n}x_{j}\frac{k!}{\prod_{i^{\prime}=1}^{n}k_{i^{\prime}}!}\left(\prod_{i^{\prime}=1}^{n}{q_{i^{\prime}|j}}^{k_{i^{\prime}}}\right)\left(\frac{k_{i}}{k}+\mathcal{O}(\delta)\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{2x_{i}}{kN}\begin{cases}\displaystyle{\sum_{j=1,j\neq i}^{n}q_{j|i}[(k-1)q_{i|j}+1]+\mathcal{O}(\delta)},&\mbox{$i=l$,}\\
\displaystyle{\sum_{j=1,j\neq i}^{n}(k-1)q_{l|j}q_{j|i}+\mathcal{O}(\delta)},&\mbox{$i\neq l$.}\end{cases}

(S204)

In Eq. (
5.2.1
),
\mathcal{P}(j\leftarrow i)
refers to Eq. (
S173
). We only take the first-order Taylor expansion, because the first-order term would be non-zero as we will see in Eq. (
5.2.3
).

5.2.2
The decrease of
il
-edges

The number of
il
-edges in the system decreases by
k_{l}
when an
i
-player is replaced by a
j
-player (
j\neq i
). The frequency of
il
-edges
p_{il}
is decreased by
2k_{l}/(kN)
. For a possible neighbor configuration
\mathbf{k}
of the
i
-player, we go through all possible
j\neq i
, obtaining the probability that the configuration
\mathbf{k}
is found around the
i
-player and thus the frequency of
il
-edges decreases by
2k_{l}/(kN)
:

\mathcal{P}\left(\Delta p_{il}=-\frac{2k_{l}}{kN}\right)=x_{i}\frac{k!}{\prod_{i^{\prime}=1}^{n}k_{i^{\prime}}!}\left(\prod_{i^{\prime}=1}^{n}{q_{i^{\prime}|i}}^{k_{i^{\prime}}}\right)[1-\mathcal{P}(i\leftarrow i)].

(S205)

Then, we go through all possible configurations
\mathbf{k}
. The expected change in the frequency of
il
-edges is calculated as:

\displaystyle\sum_{\sum_{i^{\prime}=1}^{n}k_{i^{\prime}}=k}\left(-\frac{2k_{l}}{kN}\right)\mathcal{P}\left(\Delta p_{il}=-\frac{2k_{l}}{kN}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\sum_{i^{\prime}=1}^{n}k_{i^{\prime}}=k}\left(-\frac{2k_{l}}{kN}\right)x_{i}\frac{k!}{\prod_{i^{\prime}=1}^{n}k_{i^{\prime}}!}\left(\prod_{i^{\prime}=1}^{n}{q_{i^{\prime}|i}}^{k_{i^{\prime}}}\right)[1-\mathcal{P}(i\leftarrow i)]

\displaystyle=\sum_{\sum_{i^{\prime}=1}^{n}k_{i^{\prime}}=k}\left(-\frac{2k_{l}}{kN}\right)x_{i}\frac{k!}{\prod_{i^{\prime}=1}^{n}k_{i^{\prime}}!}\left(\prod_{i^{\prime}=1}^{n}{q_{i^{\prime}|i}}^{k_{i^{\prime}}}\right)\left(1-\frac{k_{i}}{k}+\mathcal{O}(\delta)\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{2x_{i}}{kN}\begin{cases}\displaystyle{kq_{i|i}-q_{i|i}[(k-1)q_{i|i}+1]+\mathcal{O}(\delta)},&\mbox{$i=l$,}\\
\displaystyle{kq_{l|i}-(k-1)q_{l|i}q_{i|i}+\mathcal{O}(\delta)},&\mbox{$i\neq l$.}\end{cases}

(S206)

In Eq. (
5.2.2
),
[1-\mathcal{P}(i\leftarrow i)]
refers to Eq. (
S175
). Again, we only take the first-order Taylor expansion, because the first-order term would be non-zero as we will see in Eq. (
5.2.3
).

5.2.3
Separation of different time scales

We combine the increase and decrease of
il
-edges given by Eqs. (
5.2.1
) and (
5.2.2
), and consider that a full MC step contains
N
elementary steps. In this way, we have the master equation of the change in the
il
-edge frequency under death-birth:

\displaystyle\dot{p}_{il}

\displaystyle=N\times\left(\sum_{\sum_{i^{\prime}=1}^{n}k_{i^{\prime}}=k}\frac{2k_{l}}{kN}\mathcal{P}\left(\Delta p_{il}=\frac{2k_{l}}{kN}\right)+\sum_{\sum_{i^{\prime}=1}^{n}k_{i^{\prime}}=k}\left(-\frac{2k_{l}}{kN}\right)\mathcal{P}\left(\Delta p_{il}=-\frac{2k_{l}}{kN}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{2x_{i}}{k}\left(\theta_{il}+(k-1)\sum_{j=1}^{n}q_{l|j}q_{j|i}-kq_{l|i}\right)+\mathcal{O}(\delta),

(S207)

where
\theta_{il}=1
if
i=l
and
\theta_{il}=0
otherwise.

According to Eq. (
1.1
), we consider
q_{l|i}=p_{il}/x_{i}
and write
\dot{q}_{l|i}
by
\dot{p}_{il}
as follows.

\dot{q}_{l|i}=\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\left(\frac{p_{il}}{x_{i}}\right)=\frac{2}{k}\left(\theta_{il}+(k-1)\sum_{j=1}^{n}q_{l|j}q_{j|i}-kq_{l|i}\right)+\mathcal{O}(\delta).

(S208)

In Eq. (
S208
), we only take the first-order Taylor expansion, as the first-order term is non-zero and one can ignore the remaining higher order infinitesimals. Comparing Eqs. (
4.3
) and (
S208
), we observe that the change in
x_{i}
happens at the
\delta^{1}
scale, while the change in
q_{l|i}
happens at the
\delta^{0}
scale. The change of
q_{l|i}
are much faster than
x_{i}
.

Therefore, we can study the dynamics of
x_{i}
on the basis of
q_{l|i}
achieving equilibrium (rapidly). That is, we can solve
\dot{q}_{l|i}=0
, which is
\theta_{il}+(k-1)\sum_{j=1}^{n}q_{l|j}q_{j|i}-kq_{l|i}=0
, and represent all
q_{l|i}
quantities by
x_{i}
quantities. Considering
x_{i}q_{l|i}=x_{l}q_{i|l}
, the solution is

q_{l|i}=\frac{(k-2)x_{l}+\theta_{il}}{k-1},

(S209)

which is Eq. (
S178
) presented in Supplementary Note
4.3
, with the consideration of
\theta_{il}=1
if
i=l
and
\theta_{il}=0
otherwise.

Supplementary Note 6: Theorem on computation and operators

A summation relation central to the theoretical derivation in this work is given by Theorem
1
.

Theorem 1
.

For any real number
z_{j}
, where
j=1,2,\dots,n
, satisfying
0\leq z_{j}\leq 1
and
\sum_{j=1}^{n}z_{j}=1
, and any scalar multivariate function
g(\mathbf{k})
of
\mathbf{k}
, we have the following relation:

\sum_{\sum_{j=1}^{n}k_{j}=k}\frac{k!}{\prod_{j=1}^{n}k_{j}!}\left(\prod_{j=1}^{n}{z_{j}}^{k_{j}}\right)k_{i}g(\mathbf{k})=kz_{i}\sum_{\sum_{j=1}^{n}k_{j}=k-1}\frac{(k-1)!}{\prod_{j=1}^{n}k_{j}!}\left(\prod_{j=1}^{n}{z_{j}}^{k_{j}}\right)g(\mathbf{k}_{+i}).

(S210)

Proof.

Recall the definitions given in the main text or
Supplementary Note 1
:
\mathbf{k}=(k_{1},k_{2},\dots,k_{n})
, where
\sum_{j=1}^{n}k_{j}=k
; and
\mathbf{k}_{+i}=(k_{1},k_{2},\dots,k_{i}+1,\dots,k_{n})
, where
\sum_{j=1}^{n}k_{j}=k-1
. In this way, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{\sum_{j=1}^{n}k_{j}=k}\frac{k!}{\prod_{j=1}^{n}k_{j}!}\left(\prod_{j=1}^{n}{z_{j}}^{k_{j}}\right)k_{i}g(\mathbf{k})

\displaystyle=kz_{i}\sum_{\sum_{j=1}^{n}k_{j}=k,k_{i}\neq 0}\frac{(k-1)!}{(k_{i}-1)!\prod_{j=1,j\neq i}^{n}k_{j}!}\left({z_{i}}^{k_{i}-1}\prod_{j=1,j\neq i}^{n}{z_{j}}^{k_{j}}\right)g(\mathbf{k})

\displaystyle=kz_{i}\sum_{\sum_{j=1}^{n}k_{j}=k-1}\frac{(k-1)!}{\prod_{j=1}^{n}k_{j}!}\left(\prod_{j=1}^{n}{z_{j}}^{k_{j}}\right)g(\mathbf{k}_{+i}).

(S211)

The first step of Eq. (
1
) uses
k!=k(k-1)!
,
{z_{i}}^{k_{i}}=z_{i}{z_{i}}^{k_{i}-1}
,
k_{i}/(k_{i}!)=1/[(k_{i}-1)!]
and considers that the entire formula equals to zero when
k_{i}=0
. The second step of Eq. (
1
) is a straightforward application of the
\mathbf{k}_{+i}
concept.
∎

Theorem
1
gives a general relation which helps us eliminate
k_{i}
inside summations at the cost of transforming
g(\mathbf{k})
into
g(\mathbf{k}_{+i})
. For convenience of our readers, there are several useful applications of Theorem
1
.

For example, taking
g(\mathbf{k})=1
, we have

\sum_{\sum_{j=1}^{n}k_{j}=k}\frac{k!}{\prod_{j=1}^{n}k_{j}!}\left(\prod_{j=1}^{n}{z_{j}}^{k_{j}}\right)k_{i}=kz_{i}.

(S212)

Another example is
g(\mathbf{k})=k_{l}
. If
l=i
, we have

\sum_{\sum_{j=1}^{n}k_{j}=k}\frac{k!}{\prod_{j=1}^{n}k_{j}!}\left(\prod_{j=1}^{n}{z_{j}}^{k_{j}}\right)k_{i}k_{l}=kz_{i}\sum_{\sum_{j=1}^{n}k_{j}=k-1}\frac{(k-1)!}{\prod_{j=1}^{n}k_{j}!}\left(\prod_{j=1}^{n}{z_{j}}^{k_{j}}\right)(k_{l}+1)=kz_{i}[(k-1)z_{l}+1],

(S213)

and if
l\neq i
, it becomes

\sum_{\sum_{j=1}^{n}k_{j}=k}\frac{k!}{\prod_{j=1}^{n}k_{j}!}\left(\prod_{j=1}^{n}{z_{j}}^{k_{j}}\right)k_{i}k_{l}=kz_{i}\sum_{\sum_{j=1}^{n}k_{j}=k-1}\frac{(k-1)!}{\prod_{j=1}^{n}k_{j}!}\left(\prod_{j=1}^{n}{z_{j}}^{k_{j}}\right)k_{l}=k(k-1)z_{i}z_{l}.

(S214)

Finally, a potentially misleading example is
g(\mathbf{k})=k^{\prime}_{l}
, where
k^{\prime}_{l}\in\mathbf{k}^{\prime}\neq\mathbf{k}
(recall that
\mathbf{k}^{\prime}
is an independent variable of
\mathbf{k}
). In this way, elements in
\mathbf{k}^{\prime}
are essentially constants when studying
g(\mathbf{k})
,

\sum_{\sum_{j=1}^{n}k_{j}=k}\frac{k!}{\prod_{j=1}^{n}k_{j}!}\left(\prod_{j=1}^{n}{z_{j}}^{k_{j}}\right)k_{i}k^{\prime}_{l}=kz_{i}k^{\prime}_{l}.

(S215)

In this work, we have created many operators to convey various concepts. The main relations between these operators are summarized in Theorems
2
–
5
.

Theorem 2
.

The following equation holds:

\langle\pi_{i|j}^{\mathbf{k}_{+i}}\rangle=\langle\pi_{i}^{\mathbf{k}_{+j}}\rangle.

(S216)

Intuitively speaking, these two concepts are equivalent: (1) The expected accumulated payoff of an
i
-player neighboring a
j
-player, where the
j
-player has at least one
i
-player neighbor. (2) The expected accumulated payoff of an
i
-player, where the
i
-player has at least one
j
-player neighbor.

Proof.

Let us expand
\pi_{i|j}^{\mathbf{k}_{+i}}
according to Eq. (
S5
),

\pi_{i|j}^{\mathbf{k}_{+i}}=a_{i|\mathbf{k}_{+j}}+\sum_{\sum_{l=1}^{n}k^{\prime}_{l}=k-1}\frac{(k-1)!}{\prod_{l=1}^{n}k^{\prime}_{l}!}\left(\prod_{l=1}^{n}{q_{l|i}}^{k^{\prime}_{l}}\right)\left(a_{i|\mathbf{k}^{\prime}_{+j}}+\sum_{l=1}^{n}k^{\prime}_{l}\sum_{\sum_{\ell=1}^{n}k^{\prime\prime}_{\ell}=k-1}\frac{(k-1)!}{\prod_{\ell=1}^{n}k^{\prime\prime}_{\ell}!}\left(\prod_{\ell=1}^{n}{q_{\ell|l}}^{k^{\prime\prime}_{\ell}}\right)a_{i|\mathbf{k}^{\prime\prime}_{+l}}\right).

(S217)

By substituting Eq. (
S217
) into Eq. (
S16
), we calculate

\displaystyle\langle\pi_{i|j}^{\mathbf{k}_{+i}}\rangle=

\displaystyle\penalty\ \sum_{\sum_{i^{\prime}=1}^{n}k_{i^{\prime}}=k-1}\frac{(k-1)!}{\prod_{i^{\prime}=1}^{n}k_{i^{\prime}}!}\left(\prod_{i^{\prime}=1}^{n}{q_{{i^{\prime}}|j}}^{k_{i^{\prime}}}\right)a_{i|\mathbf{k}_{+j}}

\displaystyle\penalty\ +\sum_{\sum_{i^{\prime}=1}^{n}k_{i^{\prime}}=k-1}\frac{(k-1)!}{\prod_{i^{\prime}=1}^{n}k_{i^{\prime}}!}\left(\prod_{i^{\prime}=1}^{n}{q_{{i^{\prime}}|j}}^{k_{i^{\prime}}}\right)\sum_{\sum_{l=1}^{n}k^{\prime}_{l}=k-1}\frac{(k-1)!}{\prod_{l=1}^{n}k^{\prime}_{l}!}\left(\prod_{l=1}^{n}{q_{l|i}}^{k^{\prime}_{l}}\right)\Bigg[a_{i|\mathbf{k}^{\prime}_{+j}}

\displaystyle\penalty\ +\sum_{l=1}^{n}k^{\prime}_{l}\sum_{\sum_{\ell=1}^{n}k^{\prime\prime}_{\ell}=k-1}\frac{(k-1)!}{\prod_{\ell=1}^{n}k^{\prime\prime}_{\ell}!}\left(\prod_{\ell=1}^{n}{q_{\ell|l}}^{k^{\prime\prime}_{\ell}}\right)a_{i|\mathbf{k}^{\prime\prime}_{+l}}\Bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\penalty\ \sum_{\sum_{i^{\prime}=1}^{n}k_{i^{\prime}}=k-1}\frac{(k-1)!}{\prod_{i^{\prime}=1}^{n}k_{i^{\prime}}!}\left(\prod_{i^{\prime}=1}^{n}{q_{{i^{\prime}}|j}}^{k_{i^{\prime}}}\right)a_{i|\mathbf{k}_{+j}}+\sum_{\sum_{l=1}^{n}k^{\prime}_{l}=k-1}\frac{(k-1)!}{\prod_{l=1}^{n}k^{\prime}_{l}!}\left(\prod_{l=1}^{n}{q_{l|i}}^{k^{\prime}_{l}}\right)a_{i|\mathbf{k}^{\prime}_{+j}}

\displaystyle\penalty\ +\sum_{\sum_{l=1}^{n}k^{\prime}_{l}=k-1}\frac{(k-1)!}{\prod_{l=1}^{n}k^{\prime}_{l}!}\left(\prod_{l=1}^{n}{q_{l|i}}^{k^{\prime}_{l}}\right)\sum_{l=1}^{n}k^{\prime}_{l}\sum_{\sum_{\ell=1}^{n}k^{\prime\prime}_{\ell}=k-1}\frac{(k-1)!}{\prod_{\ell=1}^{n}k^{\prime\prime}_{\ell}!}\left(\prod_{\ell=1}^{n}{q_{\ell|l}}^{k^{\prime\prime}_{\ell}}\right)a_{i|\mathbf{k}^{\prime\prime}_{+l}}.

(S218)

Similarly, we expand
\pi_{i}^{\mathbf{k}_{+j}}
according to Eq. (
S3
),

\pi_{i}^{\mathbf{k}_{+j}}=a_{i|\mathbf{k}_{+j}}+\sum_{l=1}^{n}k_{l}\sum_{\sum_{\ell=1}^{n}k^{\prime}_{\ell}=k-1}\frac{(k-1)!}{\prod_{\ell=1}^{n}k^{\prime}_{\ell}!}\left(\prod_{\ell=1}^{n}{q_{\ell|l}}^{k^{\prime}_{\ell}}\right)a_{i|\mathbf{k}^{\prime}_{+l}}.

(S219)

By substituting Eq. (
S219
) into Eq. (
S17
), we calculate

\displaystyle\langle\pi_{i}^{\mathbf{k}_{+j}}\rangle=

\displaystyle\penalty\ \sum_{\sum_{i^{\prime}=1}^{n}k_{i^{\prime}}=k-1}\frac{(k-1)!}{\prod_{i^{\prime}=1}^{n}k_{i^{\prime}}!}\left(\prod_{i^{\prime}=1}^{n}{q_{{i^{\prime}}|i}}^{k_{i^{\prime}}}\right)\pi_{i}^{\mathbf{k}_{+j}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\penalty\ \sum_{\sum_{i^{\prime}=1}^{n}k_{i^{\prime}}=k-1}\frac{(k-1)!}{\prod_{i^{\prime}=1}^{n}k_{i^{\prime}}!}\left(\prod_{i^{\prime}=1}^{n}{q_{{i^{\prime}}|i}}^{k_{i^{\prime}}}\right)a_{i|\mathbf{k}_{+j}}+\sum_{\sum_{\ell=1}^{n}k^{\prime}_{\ell}=k-1}\frac{(k-1)!}{\prod_{\ell=1}^{n}k^{\prime}_{\ell}!}\left(\prod_{\ell=1}^{n}{q_{\ell|j}}^{k^{\prime}_{\ell}}\right)a_{i|\mathbf{k}^{\prime}_{+j}}

\displaystyle\penalty\ +\sum_{\sum_{i^{\prime}=1}^{n}k_{i^{\prime}}=k-1}\frac{(k-1)!}{\prod_{i^{\prime}=1}^{n}k_{i^{\prime}}!}\left(\prod_{i^{\prime}=1}^{n}{q_{{i^{\prime}}|i}}^{k_{i^{\prime}}}\right)\sum_{l=1}^{n}k_{l}\sum_{\sum_{\ell=1}^{n}k^{\prime}_{\ell}=k-1}\frac{(k-1)!}{\prod_{\ell=1}^{n}k^{\prime}_{\ell}!}\left(\prod_{\ell=1}^{n}{q_{\ell|l}}^{k^{\prime}_{\ell}}\right)a_{i|\mathbf{k}^{\prime}_{+l}}.

(S220)

Comparing Eqs. (
2
) and (
2
) and realizing the equivalence of auxiliary variables, we have
\langle\pi_{i|j}^{\mathbf{k}_{+i}}\rangle=\langle\pi_{i}^{\mathbf{k}_{+j}}\rangle
.
∎

Theorem 3
.

The following equation holds:

\sum_{j=1}^{n}q_{j|i}\langle\pi_{i|j}^{\mathbf{k}_{+i}}\rangle=\langle\pi_{i}^{\mathbf{k}}\rangle.

(S221)

Intuitive interpretation: (1) the expected accumulated payoff of an
i
-player over all possible neighboring
j
-players found near an
i
-player, where the
j
-player has at least one
i
-player neighbor, is equivalent to (2) the expected accumulated payoff of an
i
-player.

Proof.

Further developing Eq. (
2
) results in

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{n}q_{j|i}\langle\pi_{i|j}^{\mathbf{k}_{+i}}\rangle=

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{n}q_{j|i}\sum_{\sum_{i^{\prime}=1}^{n}k_{i^{\prime}}=k-1}\frac{(k-1)!}{\prod_{i^{\prime}=1}^{n}k_{i^{\prime}}!}\left(\prod_{i^{\prime}=1}^{n}{q_{{i^{\prime}}|j}}^{k_{i^{\prime}}}\right)a_{i|\mathbf{k}_{+j}}+\sum_{j=1}^{n}q_{j|i}\sum_{\sum_{l=1}^{n}k^{\prime}_{l}=k-1}\frac{(k-1)!}{\prod_{l=1}^{n}k^{\prime}_{l}!}\left(\prod_{l=1}^{n}{q_{l|i}}^{k^{\prime}_{l}}\right)a_{i|\mathbf{k}^{\prime}_{+j}}

\displaystyle+\sum_{j=1}^{n}q_{j|i}\sum_{\sum_{l=1}^{n}k^{\prime}_{l}=k-1}\frac{(k-1)!}{\prod_{l=1}^{n}k^{\prime}_{l}!}\left(\prod_{l=1}^{n}{q_{l|i}}^{k^{\prime}_{l}}\right)\sum_{l=1}^{n}k^{\prime}_{l}\sum_{\sum_{\ell=1}^{n}k^{\prime\prime}_{\ell}=k-1}\frac{(k-1)!}{\prod_{\ell=1}^{n}k^{\prime\prime}_{\ell}!}\left(\prod_{\ell=1}^{n}{q_{\ell|l}}^{k^{\prime\prime}_{\ell}}\right)a_{i|\mathbf{k}^{\prime\prime}_{+l}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{n}q_{j|i}\sum_{\sum_{i^{\prime}=1}^{n}k_{i^{\prime}}=k-1}\frac{(k-1)!}{\prod_{i^{\prime}=1}^{n}k_{i^{\prime}}!}\left(\prod_{i^{\prime}=1}^{n}{q_{{i^{\prime}}|j}}^{k_{i^{\prime}}}\right)a_{i|\mathbf{k}_{+j}}+\sum_{j=1}^{n}\frac{k_{j}}{k}\sum_{\sum_{l=1}^{n}k^{\prime}_{l}=k}\frac{k!}{\prod_{l=1}^{n}k^{\prime}_{l}!}\left(\prod_{l=1}^{n}{q_{l|i}}^{k^{\prime}_{l}}\right)a_{i|\mathbf{k}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle+(k-1)\sum_{\sum_{l=1}^{n}k^{\prime}_{l}=k-2}\frac{(k-2)!}{\prod_{l=1}^{n}k^{\prime}_{l}!}\left(\prod_{l=1}^{n}{q_{l|i}}^{k^{\prime}_{l}}\right)\sum_{l=1}^{n}q_{l|i}\sum_{\sum_{\ell=1}^{n}k^{\prime\prime}_{\ell}=k-1}\frac{(k-1)!}{\prod_{\ell=1}^{n}k^{\prime\prime}_{\ell}!}\left(\prod_{\ell=1}^{n}{q_{\ell|l}}^{k^{\prime\prime}_{\ell}}\right)a_{i|\mathbf{k}^{\prime\prime}_{+l}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{n}q_{j|i}\sum_{\sum_{i^{\prime}=1}^{n}k_{i^{\prime}}=k-1}\frac{(k-1)!}{\prod_{i^{\prime}=1}^{n}k_{i^{\prime}}!}\left(\prod_{i^{\prime}=1}^{n}{q_{{i^{\prime}}|j}}^{k_{i^{\prime}}}\right)a_{i|\mathbf{k}_{+j}}+\sum_{\sum_{l=1}^{n}k^{\prime}_{l}=k}\frac{k!}{\prod_{l=1}^{n}k^{\prime}_{l}!}\left(\prod_{l=1}^{n}{q_{l|i}}^{k^{\prime}_{l}}\right)a_{i|\mathbf{k}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle+(k-1)\sum_{l=1}^{n}q_{l|i}\sum_{\sum_{\ell=1}^{n}k^{\prime\prime}_{\ell}=k-1}\frac{(k-1)!}{\prod_{\ell=1}^{n}k^{\prime\prime}_{\ell}!}\left(\prod_{\ell=1}^{n}{q_{\ell|l}}^{k^{\prime\prime}_{\ell}}\right)a_{i|\mathbf{k}^{\prime\prime}_{+l}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\sum_{l=1}^{n}k^{\prime}_{l}=k}\frac{k!}{\prod_{l=1}^{n}k^{\prime}_{l}!}\left(\prod_{l=1}^{n}{q_{l|i}}^{k^{\prime}_{l}}\right)a_{i|\mathbf{k}^{\prime}}+k\sum_{l=1}^{n}q_{l|i}\sum_{\sum_{\ell=1}^{n}k^{\prime\prime}_{\ell}=k-1}\frac{(k-1)!}{\prod_{\ell=1}^{n}k^{\prime\prime}_{\ell}!}\left(\prod_{\ell=1}^{n}{q_{\ell|l}}^{k^{\prime\prime}_{\ell}}\right)a_{i|\mathbf{k}^{\prime\prime}_{+l}}.

(S222)

On the other hand, we apply the expression of
\pi_{i}^{\mathbf{k}}
in Eq. (
S3
) to Eq. (
S17
) and calculate

\displaystyle\langle\pi_{i}^{\mathbf{k}}\rangle=

\displaystyle\penalty\ \sum_{\sum_{i^{\prime}=1}^{n}k_{i^{\prime}}=k}\frac{k!}{\prod_{i^{\prime}=1}^{n}k_{i^{\prime}}!}\left(\prod_{i^{\prime}=1}^{n}{q_{{i^{\prime}}|i}}^{k_{i^{\prime}}}\right)\pi_{i}^{\mathbf{k}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\penalty\ \sum_{\sum_{i^{\prime}=1}^{n}k_{i^{\prime}}=k}\frac{k!}{\prod_{i^{\prime}=1}^{n}k_{i^{\prime}}!}\left(\prod_{i^{\prime}=1}^{n}{q_{{i^{\prime}}|i}}^{k_{i^{\prime}}}\right)a_{i|\mathbf{k}}

\displaystyle\penalty\ +\sum_{\sum_{i^{\prime}=1}^{n}k_{i^{\prime}}=k}\frac{k!}{\prod_{i^{\prime}=1}^{n}k_{i^{\prime}}!}\left(\prod_{i^{\prime}=1}^{n}{q_{{i^{\prime}}|i}}^{k_{i^{\prime}}}\right)\sum_{l=1}^{n}k_{l}\sum_{\sum_{\ell=1}^{n}k^{\prime}_{\ell}=k-1}\frac{(k-1)!}{\prod_{\ell=1}^{n}k^{\prime}_{\ell}!}\left(\prod_{\ell=1}^{n}{q_{\ell|l}}^{k^{\prime}_{\ell}}\right)a_{i|\mathbf{k}^{\prime}_{+l}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\penalty\ \sum_{\sum_{i^{\prime}=1}^{n}k_{i^{\prime}}=k}\frac{k!}{\prod_{i^{\prime}=1}^{n}k_{i^{\prime}}!}\left(\prod_{i^{\prime}=1}^{n}{q_{{i^{\prime}}|i}}^{k_{i^{\prime}}}\right)a_{i|\mathbf{k}}+k\sum_{l=1}^{n}q_{l|i}\sum_{\sum_{\ell=1}^{n}k^{\prime}_{\ell}=k-1}\frac{(k-1)!}{\prod_{\ell=1}^{n}k^{\prime}_{\ell}!}\left(\prod_{\ell=1}^{n}{q_{\ell|l}}^{k^{\prime}_{\ell}}\right)a_{i|\mathbf{k}^{\prime}_{+l}}.

(S223)

Comparing Eqs. (
3
) and (
3
) and noting that

\mathbf{k}^{\prime}
,
\mathbf{k}^{\prime\prime}_{+l}
,
\mathbf{k}
, and
\mathbf{k}^{\prime}_{+l}
are calculated separately here (i.e., we can treat
\mathbf{k}^{\prime}
,
\mathbf{k}
as
\mathbf{k}
and treat
\mathbf{k}^{\prime\prime}_{+l}
,
\mathbf{k}^{\prime}_{+l}
as
\mathbf{k}_{+l}
), we have
\sum_{j=1}^{n}q_{j|i}\langle\pi_{i|j}^{\mathbf{k}_{+i}}\rangle=\langle\pi_{i}^{\mathbf{k}}\rangle
.
∎

Theorem 4
.

The following equation holds:

\langle a_{i|\mathbf{k}}\rangle_{i}=\sum_{j=1}^{n}q_{j|i}\langle a_{i|\mathbf{k}_{+j}}\rangle_{i}.

(S224)

Intuitive interpretation: (1) the expected single-game payoff of an
i
-player is equivalent to (2) the expected single-game payoff of an
i
-player conditioning on all possible neighboring
j
-players, where the
i
-player has at least one
j
-player neighbor.

Proof.

According to the definition of
\langle a_{i|\mathbf{k}}\rangle_{j}
in Eq. (
S21
), we have

\displaystyle\langle a_{i|\mathbf{k}}\rangle_{i}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\sum_{i^{\prime}=1}^{n}k_{i^{\prime}}=k}\frac{k!}{\prod_{i^{\prime}=1}^{n}k_{i^{\prime}}!}\left(\prod_{i^{\prime}=1}^{n}{q_{{i^{\prime}}|i}}^{k_{i^{\prime}}}\right)a_{i|\mathbf{k}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{n}\sum_{\sum_{i^{\prime}=1}^{n}k_{i^{\prime}}=k}\frac{k!}{\prod_{i^{\prime}=1}^{n}k_{i^{\prime}}!}\left(\prod_{i^{\prime}=1}^{n}{q_{{i^{\prime}}|i}}^{k_{i^{\prime}}}\right)\frac{k_{j}}{k}a_{i|\mathbf{k}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{n}q_{j|i}\sum_{\sum_{i^{\prime}=1}^{n}k_{i^{\prime}}=k-1}\frac{(k-1)!}{\prod_{i^{\prime}=1}^{n}k_{i^{\prime}}!}\left(\prod_{i^{\prime}=1}^{n}{q_{{i^{\prime}}|i}}^{k_{i^{\prime}}}\right)a_{i|\mathbf{k}_{+j}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{n}q_{j|i}\langle a_{i|\mathbf{k}_{+j}}\rangle_{i},

(S225)

which completes the proof.
∎

Theorem 5
.

The following equation holds:

\sum_{j=1}^{n}\sum_{i^{\prime}=1}^{n}q_{i^{\prime}|j}q_{j|i}\langle\pi_{i|j}^{\mathbf{k}_{+i,+i^{\prime}}}\rangle=\langle\pi_{i}^{\mathbf{k}}\rangle.

(S226)

Intuitive interpretation: (1) the expected accumulated payoff of an
i
-player over all possible neighboring
j
-players found near an
i
-player and all possible neighboring
i^{\prime}
-players found near an
j
-player, where the
j
-player has at least one
i
-player neighbor and one
i^{\prime}
-player neighbor, is equivalent to (2) the expected accumulated payoff of an
i
-player.

Proof.

According to Theorem
3
, we have
\langle\pi_{i}^{\mathbf{k}}\rangle=\sum_{j=1}^{n}q_{j|i}\langle\pi_{i|j}^{\mathbf{k}_{+i}}\rangle
. Furthermore, we calculate

\displaystyle\langle\pi_{i|j}^{\mathbf{k}_{+i}}\rangle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\sum_{i^{\prime}=1}^{n}k_{i^{\prime}}=k-1}\frac{(k-1)!}{\prod_{i^{\prime}=1}^{n}k_{i^{\prime}}!}\left(\prod_{i^{\prime}=1}^{n}{q_{{i^{\prime}}|j}}^{k_{i^{\prime}}}\right)\pi_{i|j}^{\mathbf{k}_{+i}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\sum_{i^{\prime}=1}^{n}k_{i^{\prime}}=k-1}\frac{(k-1)!}{\prod_{i^{\prime}=1}^{n}k_{i^{\prime}}!}\left(\prod_{i^{\prime}=1}^{n}{q_{{i^{\prime}}|j}}^{k_{i^{\prime}}}\right)\sum_{i^{\prime}=1}^{n}\frac{k_{i^{\prime}}}{k-1}\pi_{i|j}^{\mathbf{k}_{+i}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\sum_{i^{\prime}=1}^{n}k_{i^{\prime}}=k-2}\frac{(k-2)!}{\prod_{i^{\prime}=1}^{n}k_{i^{\prime}}!}\left(\prod_{i^{\prime}=1}^{n}{q_{{i^{\prime}}|j}}^{k_{i^{\prime}}}\right)\sum_{i^{\prime}=1}^{n}q_{i^{\prime}|j}\pi_{i|j}^{\mathbf{k}_{+i,+i^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{i^{\prime}=1}^{n}q_{i^{\prime}|j}\langle\pi_{i|j}^{\mathbf{k}_{+i,+i^{\prime}}}\rangle.

(S227)

Applying Eq. (
5
) to Theorem
3
, we obtain
\langle\pi_{i}^{\mathbf{k}}\rangle=\sum_{j=1}^{n}\sum_{i^{\prime}=1}^{n}q_{i^{\prime}|j}q_{j|i}\langle\pi_{i|j}^{\mathbf{k}_{+i,+i^{\prime}}}\rangle
, which completes the proof.
∎
